```text
      │
      ├── Manual compilation
      ├── Editorial review
      ├── Comics
      ├── Videos
      ├── Audio
      ├── 4–5 Service Cards
      └── Publication and archive
```

# 3. Three technical refinements I recommend

Before approving this protocol, there are three details worth resolving.

| Issue               | Recommendation                                                                               |
| ------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Millennium Probe    | Treat `now_iso` as the production clock and `probe_iso` as the fictional temporal reference. |
| Continuity          | Maintain a compact, approved continuity record that can be transferred between AI providers. |
| Challenge solutions | Keep solutions in a restricted editorial record, separate from public-facing chronicles.     |

**One additional calendar consideration:** The four-episode-per-chapter calculation assumes that the chapter contains enough calendar days to accommodate four complete Monday-to-Friday, two-week episodes. The protocol correctly requires discrepancies to be reported rather than silently extending an episode beyond its phase.

# 4. Recommended next document

I recommend developing the **Classies Chronicles Episode Continuity Ledger & Production Manifest (CC-ECLPM)** next.

This is arguably the most important missing operational component.

It would serve two purposes:

* **Continuity Ledger:** Records what has happened, character developments, outstanding missions, mysteries and challenge solutions.
* **Production Manifest:** Tracks the ten chronicles, their approval status, comics, videos, audio, Service Cards and publication readiness.

Together, these three documents would establish a reusable production system:

1. Master Framework — defines the universe and production rules.
2. Daily Generation Protocol — tells the AI what to do each day.
3. Continuity Ledger & Production Manifest — remembers what has happened and tracks what remains to be produced.

This would make Classies Chronicles considerably easier to operate across multiple AI providers without losing narrative consistency.

Develop the Classies Chronicles Episode Continuity Ledger & Production Manifest (CC-ECLPM)  

The **Classies Chronicles Episode Continuity Ledger & Production Manifest (CC-ECLPM)** will be the third operational document in the Classies Chronicles production architecture.

Its responsibility is particularly important: ensuring that the storyline remains consistent across ten working days, different AI providers, human editors and multiple production formats.

I recommend designing it as a combined document with two distinct but interconnected components:

1. **Episode Continuity Ledger (ECL):** Remembers what has happened and what must happen next.
2. **Episode Production Manifest (EPM):** Tracks what needs to be generated, reviewed, approved, edited, archived and published.

The two components should share identifiers but maintain separate responsibilities.

Below is the proposed document.

# CLASSIES CHRONICLES

## EPISODE CONTINUITY LEDGER & PRODUCTION MANIFEST

**Document Reference:** CC-ECLPM-0.01.00
**Version:** 0.01.00
**Status:** Proposed — For Review and Approval
**Document Type:** Narrative Continuity and Production Management Framework
**Parent Document:** Classies Chronicles Master Episode & Daily Chronicle Generation Framework
**Operational Companion:** Classies Chronicles Daily Chronicle Generation Protocol
**Production Model:** Ten Working Days per Episode
**Compatibility:** Provider-Independent AI Systems

---

# PART A — GENERAL FRAMEWORK

# 1. PURPOSE

## 1.1 Objective

The Classies Chronicles Episode Continuity Ledger & Production Manifest establishes a standardised mechanism for maintaining narrative continuity and managing the production lifecycle of every Classies Chronicles episode.

It addresses two fundamental requirements:

1. The fictional universe must remember what has happened.
2. The production environment must know what has been created, what remains outstanding and what is ready for publication.

The system must operate independently of any particular AI provider, production application or editing platform.

It must support collaboration between ChatGPT, Claude, Copilot, Perplexity, human editors and other authorised production contributors.

## 1.2 Core responsibilities

The CC-ECLPM is responsible for:

* Episode identification.
* Daily chronicle tracking.
* Character continuity.
* Storyline continuity.
* Edubeam mission tracking.
* Millennium Timezone information.
* Organisational developments.
* Research references.
* Educational continuity.
* Audience challenge management.
* Challenge solution management.
* Media production tracking.
* Editorial approval.
* Publishing readiness.
* Episode archival.
* Handover to subsequent episodes.

## 1.3 Governing principle

The continuity ledger and production manifest must never be treated as interchangeable.

The continuity ledger answers:

**WHAT HAS HAPPENED, WHY DID IT HAPPEN AND WHAT SHOULD HAPPEN NEXT?**

The production manifest answers:

**WHAT HAS BEEN PRODUCED, WHAT IS STILL REQUIRED AND WHAT IS READY FOR RELEASE?**

Both are necessary for reliable production.

---

# PART B — EPISODE CONTINUITY LEDGER

# 2. CONTINUITY LEDGER ARCHITECTURE

## 2.1 General principles

Each episode must maintain its own continuity ledger.

The ledger must preserve the narrative information necessary to continue the storyline without requiring the next AI or editor to reread every previous chronicle.

It must contain sufficient information to reconstruct the episode's important developments.

It should not become a duplicate archive of the entire episode.

Detailed source materials, complete chronicles, videos and other media remain in their designated episode archives.

## 2.2 Continuity hierarchy

The continuity system operates at five levels.

LEVEL 1 — FRANCHISE CANON

Information applicable across the entire Classies Chronicles universe.

Examples:

* Established characters.
* Character identities.
* Character histories.
* Relationships.
* Fictional universe rules.
* Established technological capabilities.
* Millennium Timezone rules.
* Edubeam principles.
* Historical and future settings.

LEVEL 2 — SEASON CONTINUITY

Information relevant to the overall annual storyline.

Examples:

* Major annual objectives.
* Long-running mysteries.
* Season-wide character development.
* Important organisational developments.
* Major technological projects.

LEVEL 3 — CHAPTER CONTINUITY

Information applicable to the eight-week chapter.

Examples:

* Chapter objectives.
* Major assignments.
* Continuing investigations.
* Character developments.
* Organisational themes.
* Outstanding chapter-level matters.

LEVEL 4 — EPISODE CONTINUITY

Information applicable to the ten-working-day episode.

Examples:

* Episode storyline.
* Mission objectives.
* Character participation.
* Important discoveries.
* Unresolved events.
* Episode challenges.

LEVEL 5 — DAILY CHRONICLE CONTINUITY

Information applicable to an individual working day.

Examples:

* Daily events.
* Character interactions.
* Discoveries.
* Decisions.
* Dialogue developments.
* Daily challenge.
* Instructions for the next chronicle.

Information should flow between these levels without automatically promoting every new fictional development into permanent franchise canon.

## 2.3 Canon status

Every significant fictional development must have an identifiable status.

Use the following classifications:

ESTABLISHED CANON

Previously approved information.

PROPOSED DEVELOPMENT

New information generated by an AI or contributor but not yet approved.

APPROVED DEVELOPMENT

New information formally accepted into the storyline.

HISTORICAL RECORD

Information documenting an event that has already occurred.

ALTERNATIVE PROPOSAL

An alternative storyline or interpretation that has not been adopted.

SUPERSEDED

Information replaced by a subsequently approved development.

REJECTED

Information explicitly declined by the editorial authority.

An AI must not treat proposed developments as established canon.

---

Every continuity ledger must begin with an episode identification record.

Recommended structure:

The episode identifier must remain stable throughout production.

A proposed episode title may change during editorial review without changing the episode identifier.

---

# 4. DAILY CONTINUITY REGISTER

## 4.1 Purpose

The Daily Continuity Register provides a chronological record of the ten daily chronicles.

It allows an AI to establish the narrative position immediately before generating the next chronicle.

## 4.2 Register structure

Maintain one entry for each working day.

| Day | Weekday   | Chronicle | Status   |
| --- | --------- | --------- | -------- |
| 01  | Monday    | [Title]   | [Status] |
| 02  | Tuesday   | [Title]   | [Status] |
| 03  | Wednesday | [Title]   | [Status] |
| 04  | Thursday  | [Title]   | [Status] |
| 05  | Friday    | [Title]   | [Status] |
| 06  | Monday    | [Title]   | [Status] |
| 07  | Tuesday   | [Title]   | [Status] |
| 08  | Wednesday | [Title]   | [Status] |
| 09  | Thursday  | [Title]   | [Status] |
| 10  | Friday    | [Title]   | [Status] |

## 4.3 Daily event record

Every chronicle must have a corresponding event record.

DAILY EVENT RECORD

[Unique identifier]

[1–10]

Editorial Status:

The record should be concise but sufficiently detailed to preserve narrative continuity.

## 4.4 Chronological integrity

Events must remain in their correct chronological sequence.

Where an episode contains multiple timelines, maintain separate timeline references.

For example:

* Actual production timeline.
* Millennium probing timeline.
* Historical events.
* Flashbacks.
* Simulated environments.

Never merge events occurring in different temporal contexts without an explicit narrative explanation.

---

# 5. CHARACTER CONTINUITY REGISTER

## 5.1 Purpose

The Character Continuity Register tracks the development and circumstances of recurring characters.

It prevents inconsistent personalities, relationships, locations, appearances and character histories.

## 5.2 Character record

## 5.3 Character development tracking

Record meaningful changes in:

* Personal motivations.
* Knowledge.
* Skills.
* Relationships.
* Responsibilities.
* Experiences.
* Emotional development.
* Beliefs.
* Personal objectives.

Do not introduce arbitrary personality changes.

A character's development should be traceable to actual narrative events.

## 5.4 Character appearance continuity

Where visual production is involved, reference approved character images and descriptions.

Track:

* Clothing.
* Hairstyle.
* Physical attributes.
* Accessories.
* Equipment.
* Age representation.
* Relevant injuries or temporary physical changes.

The approved character reference should remain authoritative for subsequent comic and video generation.

---

# 6. STORYLINE AND PLOT CONTINUITY

## 6.1 Storyline register

Every significant storyline must have a unique identifier.

STORYLINE RECORD

Storyline ID:
[Identifier]

Storyline Title:
[Title]

Origin:
[Chronicle or episode]

Description:
[Summary]

[List]

Relevant Locations:
[List]

[Personal / Organisational / Scientific / Historical / Technological / Mystery / Other]

[Status]

[List]

[List]

[List]

[Conditions]

## 6.2 Storyline status

Use the following statuses:

* Proposed.
* Introduced.
* Active.
* Developing.
* Complicated.
* Awaiting Investigation.
* Awaiting Decision.
* Resolved.
* Suspended.
* Abandoned.
* Archived.

A storyline may remain active across several episodes or seasons.

## 6.3 Mystery management

Mysteries and investigative storylines require additional tracking.

Record:

* Original mystery.
* Known facts.
* Suspected explanations.
* Verified discoveries.
* Unverified theories.
* Characters aware of the mystery.
* Information deliberately withheld.
* Possible resolution paths.

Do not allow an AI to reveal a mystery's solution prematurely.

## 6.4 Narrative dependencies

Some storylines depend on earlier events.

Record these dependencies explicitly.

For example:

STORYLINE A:
A laboratory anomaly is detected.

STORYLINE B:
The team investigates the anomaly.

STORYLINE C:
The investigation reveals an unexpected historical connection.

STORYLINE D:
The discovery creates a new organisational challenge.

STORYLINE B must not be treated as complete before Storyline A has occurred.

---

# 7. EDUBEAM AND MILLENNIUM MISSION CONTINUITY

## 7.1 Purpose

The Edubeam Mission Register maintains the operational and narrative history of temporal assignments.

It ensures that the team, equipment, mission objectives and discoveries remain consistent.

## 7.2 Mission record

## 7.3 Mission status

* In Progress.
* Communication Interrupted.
* Anomaly Detected.
* Retrieval Pending.
* Returning.
* Completed.
* Suspended.
* Aborted.

## 7.4 Mission continuity rules

The AI must:

1. Identify whether an existing mission is active.
2. Retrieve its latest approved status.
3. Establish which characters are deployed.
4. Establish which personnel remain at the laboratory.
5. Review equipment availability.
6. Review communications.
7. Identify outstanding objectives.
8. Determine whether the mission should continue, change or conclude.

A new mission must not automatically replace an unfinished mission.

Mission changes must have an identifiable narrative reason.

## 7.5 Temporal information

Preserve the distinction between:

* Actual production timestamp.
* Millennium Probe timestamp.
* Historical destination date.
* Mission commencement.
* Mission duration.
* Return timestamp.

All timestamps should use ISO 8601 where possible.

The probe endpoint remains the authoritative source for its returned temporal information.

## 9.3 Health & Safety

Record:

* Course identifier.
* Course title.
* Lesson identifier.
* Learning objective.
* Safety principle.
* Practical application.
* Chronicle reference.
* Source material.

## 9.4 Educational continuity

The same educational subject may develop across several chronicles.

For example:

A Health & Safety principle introduced on one day may be demonstrated practically on a subsequent day.

The ledger should identify these connections.

The AI must avoid presenting repeated material as a new lesson unless the narrative requires reinforcement.

## 10.2 Source integrity

Distinguish between:

* Verified real-world information.
* Historical facts.
* Scientific findings.
* Fictional events.
* Future projections.
* AI-generated speculation.

The AI must not transform fictional developments into purported real-world news.

Research records should preserve source references so subsequent editors can verify the material.

---

# 11. AUDIENCE CHALLENGE REGISTER

## 11.1 Purpose

The Audience Challenge Register manages challenges introduced throughout the ten chronicles.

Every daily chronicle must conclude with a challenge.

## 11.2 Challenge record

[Biblical / Logical / Scientific / Historical / Health & Safety / Technology / General]

[Exact wording]

[Restricted]

[Where applicable]

[Objective]

[Date, when established]

[Subsequent episode]

[Pending / Approved / Released]

Editorial Approval:
[Status]

## 11.3 Challenge security

The public-facing challenge and internal solution must be maintained separately.

The challenge solution must not appear in:

* Public chronicles.
* Public comics.
* Public videos.
* Public social media extracts.

The solution should be released with the subsequent episode.

The archive must retain the original challenge and its approved answer.

## 11.4 Audience participation

Where audience responses are collected, the register may additionally record:

* Submission identifier.
* Submission date.
* Response.
* Evaluation status.
* Recognition or reward status.

Personal information must be handled in accordance with applicable consent and privacy requirements.

---

# PART C — EPISODE PRODUCTION MANIFEST

# 12. PRODUCTION MANIFEST ARCHITECTURE

## 12.1 Purpose

The Production Manifest tracks the complete production lifecycle of an episode.

It records every required deliverable and its current status.

The manifest should prevent:

* Missing chronicles.
* Duplicate production.
* Unapproved media.
* Forgotten editorial tasks.
* Incomplete challenges.
* Missing Service Cards.
* Unarchived production files.
* Premature publication.

## 12.2 Production units

The manifest must recognise three production levels.

LEVEL 1 — EPISODE

The complete ten-day episode.

LEVEL 2 — DAILY CHRONICLE

Each of the ten individual instalments.

LEVEL 3 — DERIVATIVE MEDIA

Comics, illustrations, videos, audio, social media content and Service Cards.

Every deliverable must have a unique identifier and a relationship to its originating episode or chronicle.

---

# 15. DERIVATIVE MEDIA PRODUCTION TRACKER

## 15.1 General principle

Every derivative media asset must reference its originating chronicle.

This creates traceability between the original narrative and the resulting media.

## 15.2 Comic production

Track:

* Comic ID.
* Episode ID.
* Chronicle ID.
* Page count.
* Panel count.
* Character references.
* Visual prompts.
* Generated artwork.
* Editing status.
* Approval status.
* Final asset location.

The comic must preserve the approved chronicle's storyline.

The audience challenge must appear at the end.

## 15.3 Video production

Track:

* Video ID.
* Episode ID.
* Chronicle ID.
* Script version.
* Scene count.
* Shot list.
* Character references.
* Video-generation prompts.
* Generated clips.
* Narration.
* Audio.
* Editing status.
* Review status.
* Final export.
* Publication status.

The video must preserve the approved storyline and conclude with the audience challenge.

## 15.4 Audio production

Track:

* Audio ID.
* Episode ID.
* Chronicle ID.
* Narration script.
* Voice reference.
* Recording.
* Editing.
* Sound effects.
* Music licensing.
* Mastering.
* Approval.
* Final asset.

## 15.5 Social media production

Track:

* Content ID.
* Source chronicle.
* Platform.
* Format.
* Caption.
* Graphics.
* Video or audio reference.
* Approval.
* Publication date.
* Publication reference.

Social media extracts must not accidentally reveal restricted challenge solutions.

---

Every completed episode should produce approximately four to five useful Service Cards.

The cards should originate from practical lessons and discoveries within the ten chronicles.

After the tenth chronicle has been generated and reviewed, the ten chronicles may proceed to manual compilation.

The compilation process must not be confused with daily generation.

# 18. EDITORIAL AND APPROVAL MANAGEMENT

## 18.1 Production statuses

Use consistent statuses across the manifest.

NOT STARTED

Work has not commenced.

IN PROGRESS

Work is underway.

DRAFT

Initial content exists.

UNDER REVIEW

Awaiting editorial review.

REVISION REQUIRED

Changes are required.

APPROVED

Formally approved.

READY FOR PRODUCTION

Approved and ready for the next production stage.

IN PRODUCTION

Derivative media is being created.

READY FOR PUBLICATION

All required publication checks are complete.

PUBLISHED

Released to the audience.

ARCHIVED

Final materials and associated records have been archived.

ON HOLD

Work is temporarily suspended.

CANCELLED

The deliverable has been formally cancelled.

## 18.2 Approval authority

AI-generated material does not automatically receive approval.

The designated human editorial authority retains responsibility for:

* Canon approval.
* Storyline approval.
* Significant character changes.
* Educational accuracy.
* Media approval.
* Consent.
* Licensing.
* Publication.

The AI may recommend approval but cannot independently declare a proposed development to be approved canon.

## 18.3 Change management

Every significant change must record:

* Change ID.
* Affected document.
* Affected chronicle.
* Previous value.
* Proposed value.
* Reason.
* Requesting party.
* Approval status.
* Approval reference.
* Implementation date.

This ensures that changes can be traced and reversed where necessary.

---

# PART D — CONTINUITY AND MANIFEST INTEGRATION

# 19. SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH

## 19.1 General principle

The CC-ECLPM should establish a consistent reference system.

However, it should not attempt to become the storage location for every production asset.

Each type of information should have an authoritative source.

| Information              | Authoritative source                       |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------ |
| Character canon          | Approved character references              |
| Storyline history        | Continuity ledger                          |
| Daily narrative          | Approved chronicle                         |
| Mission status           | Edubeam mission register                   |
| Organisational hierarchy | Approved organisational chart              |
| Bible reading plan       | Eternal Dreamfall programme                |
| Health & Safety content  | Buzzjuice course repository                |
| Production status        | Production manifest                        |
| Studio architecture      | Approved Studio Blueprint                  |
| Generated media          | Episode media archive                      |
| Challenge solutions      | Restricted challenge register              |
| Publication status       | Production manifest and publishing records |

## 19.2 Conflict resolution

When information conflicts:

1. Identify the affected records.
2. Determine their approval status.
3. Consult the authoritative source.
4. Identify the latest approved version.
5. Record the discrepancy.
6. Request clarification where necessary.
7. Update dependent records after approval.

Do not silently rewrite historical events.

## 19.3 Information duplication

The ledger may contain summaries and references.

The original source material should remain in its designated archive.

For example, the continuity ledger may record:

"Chronicle 04 establishes the laboratory anomaly."

It should reference Chronicle 04 rather than reproduce the entire chronicle.

---

# 20. AI PROVIDER HANDOVER

## 20.1 Purpose

The CC-ECLPM must allow different AI systems to work on the same episode.

A new AI provider must be able to continue the production process without relying on the previous AI's conversation memory.

## 20.2 Handover package

Whenever an AI provider changes, supply:

1. Master Episode Framework.
2. Daily Chronicle Generation Protocol.
3. Current Episode Identification.
4. Latest approved Continuity Ledger.
5. Current Production Manifest.
6. Latest approved chronicle.
7. Relevant character references.
8. Relevant mission records.
9. Outstanding editorial instructions.
10. Relevant source documents.

## 20.3 Handover instructions

The receiving AI must:

* Read the governing documents.
* Identify the current production position.
* Review approved canon.
* Review outstanding work.
* Identify the latest approved chronicle.
* Confirm the next required deliverable.
* Avoid duplicating completed work.
* Continue only after establishing the correct production position.

## 20.4 Handover summary

Prepare the following whenever a production session is concluded:

CLASSIES CHRONICLES — AI HANDOVER SUMMARY

Episode ID:
[Identifier]

[Status]

[Identifier]

[List]

[List]

## 21.1 Archive principles

* Episode records must remain identifiable.
* Approved assets must not be silently overwritten.
* Historical versions should be retained according to the archive policy.
* Restricted challenge solutions must have appropriate access controls.
* Media assets should reference their originating chronicles.
* All final production materials should be traceable to an approved episode.
* Archive changes must be documented.

# 22. IDENTIFIER AND VERSIONING CONVENTIONS

## 22.1 Unique identifiers

Use stable identifiers for production objects.

Recommended patterns:

Episode:
CC-[SEASON]-[CHAPTER]-EP[NUMBER]

Chronicle:
CC-[SEASON]-[CHAPTER]-EP[NUMBER]-D[DAY]

Character:
CC-CHAR-[IDENTIFIER]

Storyline:
CC-STORY-[IDENTIFIER]

Mission:
CC-MISSION-[IDENTIFIER]

Challenge:
CC-CHALLENGE-[IDENTIFIER]

Comic:
CC-COMIC-[EPISODE]-[CHRONICLE]

Video:
CC-VIDEO-[EPISODE]-[CHRONICLE]

Audio:
CC-AUDIO-[EPISODE]-[CHRONICLE]

Service Card:
CC-SERVICE-[EPISODE]-[NUMBER]

Production Change:
CC-CHANGE-[IDENTIFIER]

Identifiers must remain stable once assigned.

## 22.2 Versioning

Use:

MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH

Examples:

0.01.00 — Initial proposed document.

0.02.00 — Significant structural revision.

0.02.01 — Minor correction.

1.00.00 — First approved production release.

An episode's identifier should not change when its document version changes.

## 22.3 Revision history

Every managed document should contain:

* Document reference.
* Current version.
* Status.
* Revision date.
* Revision description.
* Author or contributor.
* Approval reference, where applicable.

# 23. AUTOMATION AND FUTURE INTEGRATION

## 23.1 Initial implementation

The initial implementation should remain lightweight.

The ledger and manifest may be maintained using structured text or Markdown files.

A database is not required for the initial production workflow.

## 23.2 Future integration

As the studio develops, the CC-ECLPM may be integrated with:

* CC_AI_DESK.
* Classies Chronicles repository.
* Episode archives.
* AI retrieval systems.
* Media production workflows.
* Editing applications.
* Publishing platforms.
* Buzzjuice.
* Service Card activation systems.

## 23.3 Potential automation

Future automation may support:

1. Automatic episode identification.
2. Millennium Probe retrieval.
3. Daily chronicle registration.
4. Continuity record generation.
5. Production status updates.
6. Duplicate deliverable detection.
7. Media asset registration.
8. Challenge register management.
9. Service Card tracking.
10. Episode compilation readiness.
11. AI provider handover.
12. Archive validation.

## 23.4 Data architecture recommendation

If the system eventually moves into a database, maintain separate logical entities for:

* Seasons.
* Chapters.
* Episodes.
* Chronicles.
* Characters.
* Storylines.
* Missions.
* Research sources.
* Challenges.
* Media assets.
* Service Cards.
* Production tasks.
* Approvals.
* Versions.

The database should reference the original archive assets rather than unnecessarily duplicating large media files.

A future implementation should preserve stable identifiers so existing text archives can be migrated without breaking references.

# PART F — OPERATIONAL PROCEDURES

# 24. DAILY CONTINUITY UPDATE PROCEDURE

After generating a daily chronicle:

STEP 1

Assign its unique Chronicle ID.

STEP 2

Record the actual production date and episode position.

STEP 3

Record the principal events.

STEP 4

Update the character register where necessary.

STEP 5

Update active storylines.

STEP 6

Update the Edubeam mission register where applicable.

STEP 7

Update relevant organisational developments.

STEP 8

Record Bible study and Health & Safety references.

STEP 9

Register the audience challenge.

STEP 10

Record proposed new developments.

STEP 11

Identify unresolved matters.

STEP 12

Record instructions for the next chronicle.

STEP 13

Update the Production Manifest.

STEP 14

Submit the chronicle and associated records for editorial review.

Only approved developments should be promoted into approved canon.

---

# 25. END-OF-EPISODE CONTINUITY PROCEDURE

After the tenth working day:

1. Confirm that all ten chronicles are accounted for.
2. Verify that their approved versions are identifiable.
3. Review the complete storyline.
4. Update character developments.
5. Update mission status.
6. Resolve or carry forward outstanding storylines.
7. Review organisational developments.
8. Verify educational references.
9. Finalise the challenge register.
10. Prepare the four to five Service Cards.
11. Complete the episode continuity summary.
12. Complete the production manifest.
13. Prepare the manual compilation.
14. Obtain editorial approval.
15. Archive the approved episode.
16. Prepare the next episode handover.

---

# 26. NEXT-EPISODE HANDOVER PROCEDURE

The final chronicle of an episode should provide a meaningful transition into the next episode.

The Continuity Ledger must identify:

* Resolved storylines.
* Continuing storylines.
* Outstanding mysteries.
* Character developments.
* Active or completed missions.
* Organisational developments.
* Important discoveries.
* Unresolved challenges.
* Potential future developments.

The next episode must not automatically inherit every unresolved issue.

The editorial authority determines which storylines continue.

Some may be resolved later, suspended or permanently archived.

The next episode should build upon the established universe while allowing fresh narrative development.

---

# PART H — GOVERNANCE

# 29. DOCUMENT AUTHORITY

The CC-ECLPM operates under the following document hierarchy:

LEVEL 1 — CLASSIES CHRONICLES GOVERNANCE

Approved franchise-wide policies and canon.

LEVEL 2 — MASTER EPISODE FRAMEWORK

CC-MEGF.

Defines the storytelling universe and episode structure.

LEVEL 3 — DAILY CHRONICLE GENERATION PROTOCOL

CC-DCGP.

Defines daily research and generation procedures.

LEVEL 4 — EPISODE CONTINUITY LEDGER & PRODUCTION MANIFEST

CC-ECLPM.

Defines continuity management and production tracking.

LEVEL 5 — STUDIO STRUCTURE SKELETAL BLUEPRINT

Defines the studio's organisational and production architecture.

LEVEL 6 — SPECIALIST PRODUCTION DOCUMENTS

Define specific technical, editorial, media, communications and publishing workflows.

The detailed implementation requirements of the Studio Blueprint and specialist documents remain authoritative within their respective domains.

Where a conflict occurs, the AI must identify it rather than independently inventing a resolution.

# 30. HUMAN EDITORIAL AUTHORITY

The designated human editorial authority retains final control over:

* Established canon.
* Character changes.
* Storyline approval.
* Significant mission developments.
* Episode compilation.
* Challenge solutions.
* Service Card approval.
* Media approval.
* Consent and licensing.
* Publication.

AI systems may maintain, propose and update draft records.

They must not independently approve changes that require human authorisation.

---

# 31. FINAL GOVERNING PRINCIPLE

The Episode Continuity Ledger & Production Manifest exists to ensure that Classies Chronicles develops as one coherent, persistent and manageable storytelling universe.

The Continuity Ledger remembers the story.

The Production Manifest remembers the work.

Together, they enable multiple AI systems, human contributors and production departments to collaborate without losing the narrative history or production status of the franchise.

The governing workflow is:

```
             CLASSIES CHRONICLES
                     |
         MASTER EPISODE FRAMEWORK
                     |
          DAILY GENERATION PROTOCOL
                     |
             DAILY CHRONICLE
                     |
          CONTINUITY LEDGER UPDATE
                     |
          PRODUCTION MANIFEST UPDATE
                     |
              EDITORIAL REVIEW
                     |
           TEN-CHRONICLE COMPILATION
                     |
             MEDIA PRODUCTION
                     |
            SERVICE CARD CREATION
                     |
             FINAL APPROVAL
                     |
                PUBLICATION
                     |
                  ARCHIVE
                     |
            NEXT EPISODE HANDOVER
```

The process repeats while preserving established canon and allowing the universe to evolve.

**END OF DOCUMENT**

Document Reference: CC-ECLPM-0.01.00

Version: 0.01.00

Status: Proposed — For Review and Approval.
