# 2. How the three documents now fit together

We now have three complementary operational documents.

| Document | Primary responsibility                  | Key question                                        |
| -------- | --------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| CC-MEGF  | Master Episode Framework                | What is Classies Chronicles?                        |
| CC-DCGP  | Daily Generation Protocol               | How should today's chronicle be generated?          |
| CC-ECLPM | Continuity Ledger & Production Manifest | What has happened, and what remains to be produced? |

Their relationship is:

```text
             CLASSIES CHRONICLES
                      |
             GOVERNING FRAMEWORK
                      |
          +-----------+-----------+
          |           |           |
        MEGF        DCGP        ECLPM
          |           |           |
      Story rules   Daily       Memory &
      and canon     execution   production
          |           |           |
          +-----------+-----------+
                      |
              DAILY CHRONICLE
                      |
              CONTINUITY UPDATE
                      |
              MANIFEST UPDATE
                      |
               EDITORIAL REVIEW
                      |
              TEN-DAY COMPILATION
                      |
               MEDIA PRODUCTION
                      |
                  PUBLICATION
```

# 3. Important architectural recommendation

I would make one additional distinction before implementing this in the GitHub repository.

**The CC-ECLPM should eventually become structured data rather than remaining one large text document.**

However, I would not introduce a database yet.

Start with three manageable files per episode:

| File                             | Function                              |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- |
| `episode_continuity_ledger.md`   | Narrative memory                      |
| `episode_production_manifest.md` | Production tracking                   |
| `episode_handover_summary.md`    | Context transfer between AI providers |

This avoids unnecessary complexity while establishing a migration path towards a database or integrated AI retrieval system.

The most important architectural decision is that the **continuity ledger should be updated after every approved chronicle**, not only when the ten-day episode finishes.

That gives the next AI a reliable starting point.

---

# 4. Proposed next development stage

With these three documents established, I recommend moving into a consolidation stage rather than immediately developing more independent documents.

The next deliverable should be:

**Classies Chronicles Master Production Architecture & Document Integration Specification (CC-MPADIS).**

It would reconcile:

1. Master Episode Framework.
2. Daily Chronicle Generation Protocol.
3. Episode Continuity Ledger & Production Manifest.
4. Studio Structure Skeletal Blueprint.
5. CC_AI_DESK architecture.
6. Episode archive conventions.

Its purpose would be to establish exactly which document governs each responsibility, eliminate duplicated requirements, resolve inconsistencies and define how the documents work together.

That would give Classies Chronicles a coherent production architecture before we begin automating daily chronicle generation.

Produce the Classies Chronicles Master Production Architecture & Document Integration Specification (CC-MPADIS).

The CC-MPADIS is the integration layer for the Classies Chronicles production system. Its purpose is to make the framework, daily generation protocol, continuity records, studio structure, archives and production outputs operate as one coordinated system—without merging documents that have different responsibilities.

I reviewed the original episode proposal, the compiled AI contributions and the current Classies Chronicles repository, including its published Master Episode Framework v0.01.09. One important qualification: the Studio Structure Skeletal Blueprint directory could not be retrieved through the available GitHub interface. The specification therefore identifies the studio integration points but does not pretend that its internal structure or version precedence has been fully reconciled.

The document below is a **controlled proposal, not an approved production standard**.

# CLASSIES CHRONICLES

# MASTER PRODUCTION ARCHITECTURE & DOCUMENT INTEGRATION SPECIFICATION

**Document ID:** CC-MPADIS
**Proposed Version:** 0.01.00
**Status:** Proposed — For Review and Approval
**Classification:** Master Production Architecture and Document Integration Specification
**Project:** Classies Chronicles
**Repository:** cupidblack/Classies_Chronicles
**Parent Framework:** Classies Chronicles Master Episode Framework
**Production Model:** Repository-driven, AI-independent, human-governed production
**Prepared:** 2 November 2026
**Approval Status:** Not Approved

---

# 0. DOCUMENT CONTROL

## 0.1 Purpose

This document establishes the proposed master architecture for organising, integrating, maintaining and operating the Classies Chronicles production ecosystem.

It defines how the project's governing documents, creative resources, production records, archives, research materials, AI systems, human contributors and publication outputs should work together.

The objective is to establish one coherent production environment without creating unnecessary duplication or allowing different documents to issue conflicting instructions.

## 0.2 Scope

CC-MPADIS covers:

1. Production governance and document hierarchy.
2. Integration of the Master Episode Framework.
3. Integration of the Daily Chronicle Generation Protocol.
4. Integration of the Episode Continuity Ledger and Production Manifest.
5. Integration of the Studio Structure Skeletal Blueprint.
6. Canon, character and organisational records.
7. Research and external information sources.
8. Daily chronicle generation.
9. Episode compilation and editorial review.
10. Graphic, comic, video and audio production.
11. Publication and distribution.
12. Archiving, version control and change management.
13. Future automation and system integration.

## 0.3 What this document does not authorise

This specification does not independently:

* Approve a new storyline or character development.
* Change established Classies Chronicles canon.
* Modify Koware Group's organisational structure.
* Approve changes to EduBeam or Millennium Timezone.
* Authorise access to private personal records.
* Change the existing Studio Structure Skeletal Blueprint.
* Authorise modifications to external websites or systems.
* Declare proposed production arrangements operational.

All such changes remain subject to the relevant owner and human approval process.

## 0.4 Document status definitions

| Status     | Meaning                              |
| ---------- | ------------------------------------ |
| Draft      | Being prepared                       |
| Proposed   | Submitted for consideration          |
| Candidate  | Consolidated and ready for review    |
| Approved   | Formally authorised                  |
| Active     | Approved and currently in use        |
| Superseded | Replaced by a later approved version |
| Deprecated | No longer recommended for use        |
| Archived   | Retained for historical reference    |
| Rejected   | Considered but not accepted          |

A document does not become approved merely because it has been published in a repository.

---

# 1. ARCHITECTURAL OBJECTIVE

## 1.1 The production ecosystem

Classies Chronicles should operate as a continuous creative and research ecosystem.

It is not simply a collection of independently generated stories.

Each chronicle contributes to an evolving universe while also generating material that can be developed into other production outputs.

The ecosystem combines:

* Storytelling and character development.
* Biblical study and spiritual reflection.
* Health and Safety education.
* Contemporary news and historical research.
* Fictional future developments.
* Koware Group organisational activities.
* Blue Crown research and development.
* Millennium Timezone and EduBeam narratives.
* Community participation and service cards.
* Graphic design, comics, video and audio.
* Research, innovation and potential enterprise opportunities.

These components should be integrated through a common production architecture.

## 1.2 Primary architectural principle

**One creative universe, multiple specialised production documents, one controlled continuity system.**

The architecture must avoid two extremes:

1. One enormous document containing every instruction, record and production detail.
2. Numerous disconnected documents with overlapping or contradictory responsibilities.

Instead, each document should have a defined purpose and should reference other authoritative resources where necessary.

## 1.3 Desired outcomes

The architecture should enable:

* Different AI platforms to contribute to successive chronicles.
* Human editors to maintain control over the final narrative.
* Continuity to survive between episodes and seasons.
* Creative material to be reused across multiple formats.
* Production activities to be tracked consistently.
* Research findings to remain traceable.
* Canon changes to be controlled.
* The production system to expand without requiring a complete redesign.

---

# 2. MASTER ARCHITECTURE

## 2.1 Architecture overview

The proposed production architecture consists of eight interconnected layers.

```text
CLASSIES CHRONICLES
│
├── LAYER 1 — GOVERNANCE
│   ├── Master Production Architecture
│   ├── Document Register
│   ├── Decision Register
│   └── Change Control
│
├── LAYER 2 — CREATIVE FRAMEWORK
│   ├── Master Episode Framework
│   ├── Storytelling Principles
│   ├── Narrative Structure
│   └── Production Standards
│
├── LAYER 3 — GENERATION
│   ├── Daily Chronicle Generation Protocol
│   ├── Daily Research Instructions
│   ├── AI Handover Instructions
│   └── Chronicle Output
│
├── LAYER 4 — CONTINUITY
│   ├── Episode Continuity Ledger
│   ├── Character Records
│   ├── Mission Records
│   ├── Storyline Records
│   └── Episode Handover
│
├── LAYER 5 — STUDIO AND CREATIVE RESOURCES
│   ├── Studio Structure Skeletal Blueprint
│   ├── AI Desk
│   ├── Character Canon
│   ├── Visual Identity
│   ├── Production Teams
│   └── Creative Assets
│
├── LAYER 6 — PRODUCTION MANAGEMENT
│   ├── Episode Production Manifest
│   ├── Chronicle Review
│   ├── Comic Production
│   ├── Video Production
│   ├── Audio Production
│   └── Editorial Approval
│
├── LAYER 7 — PUBLICATION AND DISTRIBUTION
│   ├── Publication Schedule
│   ├── Social Distribution
│   ├── Audience Participation
│   └── Feedback
│
└── LAYER 8 — ARCHIVES AND KNOWLEDGE
    ├── Completed Episodes
    ├── Daily Diaries
    ├── Research Archives
    ├── Historical References
    └── Production Records
```

The layers represent responsibilities, not necessarily separate software systems or repository directories.

## 2.2 Integration flow

The production process follows this general flow:

```text
GOVERNING DOCUMENTS
        │
        ▼
REFERENCE MATERIALS AND CANON
        │
        ▼
DAILY GENERATION PROTOCOL
        │
        ▼
DAILY CHRONICLE
        │
        ▼
CONTINUITY UPDATE
        │
        ▼
PRODUCTION MANIFEST UPDATE
        │
        ▼
EDITORIAL REVIEW
        │
        ▼
APPROVED EPISODE MATERIAL
        │
        ▼
COMIC / VIDEO / AUDIO / GRAPHICS
        │
        ▼
PUBLICATION
        │
        ▼
ARCHIVE AND HANDOVER
        │
        ▼
NEXT DAILY CHRONICLE OR EPISODE
```

The continuity update and production tracking are parallel responsibilities.

The Episode Continuity Ledger records what happened in the fictional universe.

The Episode Production Manifest records what has been created, reviewed, approved and published.

These records must not be treated as interchangeable.

---

# 3. DOCUMENT GOVERNANCE AND HIERARCHY

## 3.1 Proposed governing document hierarchy

The following hierarchy establishes the intended responsibilities of the principal documents.

```text
CC-MPADIS
Master Production Architecture
│
├── CC-MEGF
│   Master Episode Framework
│
├── CC-DCGP
│   Daily Chronicle Generation Protocol
│
├── CC-ECLPM
│   Episode Continuity Ledger & Production Manifest
│
├── STUDIO STRUCTURE SKELETAL BLUEPRINT
│   Studio Organisation and Production Structure
│
└── SUPPORTING AUTHORITATIVE RESOURCES
    ├── Canon and Character Records
    ├── AI Desk Architecture
    ├── Organisational Chart
    ├── Millennium Timezone References
    ├── Research Registers
    ├── Production Templates
    └── Historical Archives
```

## 3.2 Responsibility matrix

| Document             | Primary responsibility                           | Must not become                      |
| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------ |
| CC-MPADIS            | Overall architecture and integration             | Daily storytelling instructions      |
| CC-MEGF              | Creative and episode-generation framework        | Daily continuity database            |
| CC-DCGP              | Instructions for generating one chronicle        | Permanent canon repository           |
| CC-ECLPM             | Episode continuity and production tracking       | Replacement for the master framework |
| Studio Blueprint     | Studio structure and production responsibilities | Storyline or episode ledger          |
| Character Canon      | Authoritative character information              | Daily character diary                |
| AI Desk Architecture | AI roles, resources and operational arrangements | Independent canon authority          |
| Source Register      | Research and reference provenance                | Narrative continuity record          |
| Archive              | Historical production material                   | Uncontrolled source of new canon     |

## 3.3 Authority and conflict resolution

When two resources disagree, the production system must not silently select whichever information appears more convenient.

The following process applies:

1. Identify the conflicting statements.
2. Record the affected documents and versions.
3. Determine whether the conflict concerns canon, production, organisational structure or research.
4. Identify the relevant document owner or approval authority.
5. Record the proposed resolution.
6. Obtain approval where required.
7. Update the affected documents and registers.
8. Preserve the previous version and decision history.

A newer document is not automatically more authoritative than an older approved document.

Approval status, document scope and explicit supersession determine authority.

## 3.4 Existing version reconciliation

The current Classies Chronicles repository contains:

`Classies_Chronicles_Master_Episode_Framework_v0.01.09.txt`

That document identifies itself as a Refactored Functional Candidate pending human approval.

It must therefore be treated according to its recorded status, rather than automatically assumed to be an approved standard.

The earlier compiled AI proposals include recommendations for a proposed `CC-MEGF-0.02.00`, as well as alternative proposed revisions.

These historical proposals must not be confused with the currently published repository document.

The document register must record the relationship between:

* Original proposal v0.01.05.
* Compiled AI contributions.
* Repository framework v0.01.09.
* Any subsequent approved framework revision.

The exact document identifiers and version relationships must be confirmed before repository-wide renaming or supersession.

---

# 4. THE MASTER EPISODE FRAMEWORK — CC-MEGF

## 4.1 Responsibility

The Master Episode Framework defines the creative and structural rules governing Classies Chronicles.

It establishes:

* The universe and its storytelling principles.
* Narrative and episode structure.
* The annual production model.
* Daily chronicle requirements.
* Research expectations.
* Character and organisational integration.
* Educational and spiritual components.
* Fictional technology boundaries.
* Editorial and quality standards.

## 4.2 Separation of concerns

The Master Episode Framework defines what the production system should achieve.

It should not contain every daily operational instruction or every historical event.

For example:

* Character appearance belongs in character canon.
* A character's experience on a particular day belongs in the continuity ledger.
* Daily AI instructions belong in CC-DCGP.
* The status of a comic belongs in the production manifest.

## 4.3 Framework changes

A proposed change to the Master Episode Framework must be reviewed for its effects on:

* Existing canon.
* Existing episode archives.
* Daily generation instructions.
* Continuity records.
* Studio responsibilities.
* Production templates.
* Publication outputs.

A framework revision must identify which previous version it supersedes, if any.

---

# 5. DAILY CHRONICLE GENERATION PROTOCOL — CC-DCGP

## 5.1 Responsibility

CC-DCGP is the operational instruction document used whenever an AI or human-assisted production process generates a daily chronicle.

It should be reusable across compatible AI systems.

It must not be tied to a particular episode, storyline or production date.

## 5.2 Required inputs

Before generating a chronicle, the production process should identify the applicable resources.

| Input                       | Purpose                                                       |
| --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Current production date     | Establishes the real-world production position                |
| Millennium Probe            | Determines the applicable temporal phase and episode position |
| Master Episode Framework    | Supplies storytelling rules                                   |
| Previous chronicle          | Maintains immediate narrative continuity                      |
| Episode continuity ledger   | Supplies accumulated episode developments                     |
| Character canon             | Maintains established character information                   |
| Organisational chart        | Provides organisational context                               |
| Daily diary                 | Supplies authorised personal context                          |
| Bible reading plan          | Provides the applicable study material                        |
| YouVersion Verse of the Day | Provides daily scripture context                              |
| Health & Safety resources   | Supplies educational material                                 |
| News sources                | Provides verified contemporary developments                   |
| Historical archives         | Provides historical continuity                                |
| Studio resources            | Provides production-specific instructions                     |

The AI must distinguish between accessible resources and resources that were merely supplied as references.

It must not claim to have reviewed inaccessible material.

## 5.3 Generation sequence

The daily generation process should follow this order:

1. Identify the production date.
2. Query the Millennium Probe.
3. Determine the current production position.
4. Retrieve the applicable framework and generation protocol.
5. Review the previous chronicle.
6. Review the episode continuity ledger.
7. Identify active storylines and unresolved developments.
8. Review applicable character and organisational records.
9. Retrieve relevant educational and research material.
10. Conduct current-news research where required.
11. Identify the narrative opportunity for the day.
12. Generate exactly one daily chronicle.
13. Include the audience challenge.
14. Prepare the continuity update.
15. Identify the required production outputs.
16. Submit the material for human review.

## 5.4 Daily output

The daily output should contain:

* Chronicle identification.
* Production date and episode position.
* Title.
* Narrative content.
* Relevant character and organisational developments.
* Applicable educational material.
* Research references where required.
* Audience challenge.
* Continuity update.
* Outstanding questions or proposed developments.

The daily chronicle must not automatically approve its own proposed canon changes.

## 5.5 AI independence

Different AI systems may contribute to the production.

They must all follow the same governing framework and use the same approved continuity records.

No AI platform should be treated as the sole authority for canon or production decisions.

---

# 6. EPISODE CONTINUITY LEDGER AND PRODUCTION MANIFEST — CC-ECLPM

## 6.1 Responsibility

CC-ECLPM coordinates two distinct operational records.

**Episode Continuity Ledger (ECL):**

Records the evolving fictional universe.

**Episode Production Manifest (EPM):**

Records the production lifecycle and status of deliverables.

They should be governed together but maintained as separate operational records.

## 6.2 Episode Continuity Ledger

The ECL should track:

* Episode identity.
* Daily chronicle summaries.
* Character developments.
* Location changes.
* Temporal coordinates.
* Mission developments.
* Equipment and technology.
* Organisational developments.
* Important decisions.
* Discoveries.
* Unresolved mysteries.
* Audience challenges.
* Next-episode dependencies.

Each record should distinguish:

* Established canon.
* Approved development.
* Proposed development.
* Unverified information.
* Historical reference.
* Superseded information.

## 6.3 Episode Production Manifest

The EPM should track deliverables such as:

| Deliverable           | Tracking responsibility          |
| --------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Daily chronicle       | Draft, review, approval          |
| Episode compilation   | Assembly and editorial review    |
| Cover graphic         | Design and approval              |
| Episode title graphic | Design and approval              |
| Comic pages           | Script, artwork, review          |
| Video                 | Script, production, editing      |
| Trailer               | Production and approval          |
| Audio                 | Recording and editing            |
| Social content        | Preparation and publication      |
| Service cards         | Content and production           |
| Audience challenge    | Publication and solution release |
| Episode archive       | Final packaging and verification |

A deliverable must not be marked published merely because its draft exists.

## 6.4 Separate operational files

The recommended minimum operational structure for an episode is:

```text
EPISODE/
│
├── episode_continuity_ledger.md
│
├── episode_production_manifest.md
│
└── episode_handover_summary.md
```

These files serve different purposes.

The first preserves narrative continuity.

The second tracks production.

The third enables another AI, editor or production contributor to continue the work without having to reconstruct the entire episode history.

## 6.5 Episode handover

At the end of each working day, the continuity ledger and handover summary should be updated.

At the end of the tenth working day:

* The episode continuity record is reconciled.
* The production manifest is reviewed.
* Unresolved storylines are documented.
* The next episode's starting conditions are recorded.
* Restricted audience-challenge solutions remain controlled until their scheduled release.
* The final episode package is prepared for archiving.

---

# 7. STUDIO STRUCTURE INTEGRATION

## 7.1 Responsibility

The Studio Structure Skeletal Blueprint defines the organisational and functional arrangement of the studio.

CC-MPADIS should integrate with that structure rather than create a competing organisation.

The Studio Blueprint remains the reference for the studio's approved organisational arrangement, subject to its own approval status and version controls.

## 7.2 Integration requirements

The studio structure should be reconciled against the production responsibilities required by Classies Chronicles.

These responsibilities may include:

* Executive and production oversight.
* Creative direction.
* Story development.
* Research.
* Continuity management.
* AI desk coordination.
* Editorial review.
* Graphic design.
* Comic production.
* Video production.
* Audio production.
* Publication.
* Archive management.
* Audience engagement.

These are functional responsibility categories for integration purposes, not a proposal to create new departments or positions.

Existing roles should be mapped to these functions only after the authoritative Studio Blueprint has been reviewed.

## 7.3 Blueprint reconciliation requirement

The Studio Structure Skeletal Blueprint directory was identified as a required source for this specification.

However, the individual files and version history in that directory were not accessible during preparation.

Accordingly:

* No Blueprint version is declared authoritative here.
* No department or role is declared newly approved.
* No reporting relationship is invented.
* No existing studio position is superseded.
* No proposed functional mapping is treated as an approved organisational change.

**Outstanding integration task:** Obtain the Blueprint files and reconcile their versions, responsibilities, aliases and reporting relationships against this architecture.

## 7.4 Studio responsibility mapping

The eventual reconciliation should produce a mapping table:

| Production function  | Existing studio role | Blueprint reference | Status  |
| -------------------- | -------------------- | ------------------- | ------- |
| Production oversight | To be confirmed      | To be confirmed     | Pending |
| Creative direction   | To be confirmed      | To be confirmed     | Pending |
| Research             | To be confirmed      | To be confirmed     | Pending |
| Editorial            | To be confirmed      | To be confirmed     | Pending |
| Graphics             | To be confirmed      | To be confirmed     | Pending |
| Comics               | To be confirmed      | To be confirmed     | Pending |
| Video                | To be confirmed      | To be confirmed     | Pending |
| Audio                | To be confirmed      | To be confirmed     | Pending |
| Publication          | To be confirmed      | To be confirmed     | Pending |
| Archives             | To be confirmed      | To be confirmed     | Pending |

This mapping must be completed from the actual Blueprint, not from assumptions.

---

# 8. CANON AND KNOWLEDGE MANAGEMENT

## 8.1 Canon architecture

Canon should be maintained independently of daily production documents.

The proposed canon categories are:

```text
CANON
│
├── Universe Canon
│
├── Character Canon
│
├── Location Canon
│
├── Organisational Canon
│
├── Technology Canon
│
├── Temporal Canon
│
├── Historical Events
│
└── Established Relationships
```

The existing AI Desk canon resources should be retained and referenced rather than unnecessarily duplicated.

## 8.2 Character records

Each established character should have one authoritative character record.

The record may include:

* Character identity.
* Names and aliases.
* Physical appearance.
* Personality.
* Background.
* Relationships.
* Role and affiliations.
* Established abilities.
* Limitations.
* Relevant historical events.
* Visual references.

Daily character experiences belong in the continuity ledger.

## 8.3 Canon change process

A new development should pass through the following states:

```text
NEW DEVELOPMENT
       │
       ▼
PROPOSED
       │
       ▼
CONTINUITY REVIEW
       │
       ▼
HUMAN APPROVAL
       │
       ▼
CANON UPDATE
       │
       ▼
ARCHIVE AND VERSION RECORD
```

If approval is not obtained, the development remains proposed or is excluded from canon.

## 8.4 Canon conflict protection

AI-generated content must not silently overwrite:

* Character histories.
* Established relationships.
* Previously recorded events.
* Temporal coordinates.
* Technology limitations.
* Organisational structures.
* Previously approved narrative outcomes.

When a conflict is detected, the AI should identify it and request clarification rather than inventing a resolution.

---

# 9. RESEARCH AND REFERENCE ARCHITECTURE

## 9.1 Research categories

Research should be organised into distinct categories.

| Category       | Examples                                  |
| -------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| Spiritual      | Eternal Dreamfall and Bible study         |
| Educational    | Health & Safety                           |
| Contemporary   | Current news and developments             |
| Historical     | Historical events and archives            |
| Scientific     | Technology and scientific research        |
| Organisational | Koware Group structure and activities     |
| Temporal       | Millennium Timezone and Probe             |
| Creative       | Character, location and visual references |
| Community      | Audience feedback and service-card topics |

## 9.2 Source register

Research findings should be traceable to their original sources.

A source record should contain:

* Source ID.
* Source title.
* Source URL or repository location.
* Source category.
* Date accessed.
* Relevant date of the information.
* Summary of relevant material.
* Verification status.
* Associated chronicle or episode.
* Notes and limitations.

## 9.3 Real-world information versus fiction

The production architecture must distinguish between:

1. Verified real-world information.
2. Historical information.
3. Fictional events.
4. Speculative scenarios.
5. Unverified claims.

EduBeam and other fictional technologies must not be presented as established real-world science.

Future news and events must not be fabricated or reported as verified.

If information cannot be verified, its status must be stated.

## 9.4 Research reuse

A verified research item may be reused in several chronicles.

However, reuse must preserve the source, original context and applicable date.

Research should not be repeatedly collected without checking whether an existing source record already satisfies the requirement.

---

# 10. TEMPORAL AND PRODUCTION CALENDAR INTEGRATION

## 10.1 Millennium Probe

The Millennium Probe is the designated reference for determining the current temporal phase and production position.

Endpoint:

`https://buzzjuice.net/shared/millennium-timezone/millennium-probe.php?probe=now`

The generation process should retrieve the endpoint and inspect its returned fields.

Relevant fields include:

* `phase_start_iso`
* `phase_end_iso`

The endpoint must be treated as a source of returned data, not as permission to invent missing values.

The generation process should retain:

* Probe retrieval time.
* Returned phase information.
* Relevant temporal coordinates.
* Any parsing or validation warnings.

If the endpoint is unavailable or returns incomplete information, the production system must report the limitation.

It must not silently substitute a guessed production day.

## 10.2 Calendar hierarchy

The original proposal describes:

* One season per year.
* Five chapters or terms per year.
* Approximately eight weeks per chapter.
* Two weeks per episode.
* One daily chronicle per working day.

The current repository's Master Episode Framework v0.01.09 also describes an annual planning structure involving five seasons, approximately two-month seasons and approximately two-week phases.

These descriptions are not identical.

CC-MPADIS does not select one interpretation without approval.

The annual hierarchy must be reconciled against the authoritative Master Episode Framework and the existing production calendar before being encoded into automation.

## 10.3 Episode position

Once the calendar structure is confirmed, the system should identify:

* Season.
* Chapter or term.
* Episode.
* Working day.
* Calendar date.
* Episode start date.
* Episode end date.
* Phase start and end dates.

The normal ten-working-day episode structure is:

```text
WEEK 1
Monday     — Day 01
Tuesday    — Day 02
Wednesday  — Day 03
Thursday   — Day 04
Friday     — Day 05

WEEK 2
Monday     — Day 06
Tuesday    — Day 07
Wednesday  — Day 08
Thursday   — Day 09
Friday     — Day 10
```

This is the production baseline and should remain subject to any formally approved calendar exception.

---

# 11. PRODUCTION PIPELINE

## 11.1 Daily production

The daily process should be lightweight and repeatable.

```text
PREPARATION
    │
    ▼
RESOURCE REVIEW
    │
    ▼
RESEARCH
    │
    ▼
CHRONICLE GENERATION
    │
    ▼
EDITORIAL REVIEW
    │
    ▼
CONTINUITY UPDATE
    │
    ▼
PRODUCTION MANIFEST UPDATE
    │
    ▼
DAILY ARCHIVE
```

The chronicle is the primary daily creative output.

Supporting material should be derived from the approved chronicle wherever possible.

## 11.2 Episode compilation

At the end of the tenth working day, the ten daily chronicles are manually compiled and edited into one episode.

The compilation process should include:

1. Confirm all ten daily chronicles.
2. Check chronology and continuity.
3. Review character and location consistency.
4. Review the central storyline.
5. Remove unnecessary repetition.
6. Check research references.
7. Verify educational and spiritual material.
8. Review the episode's beginning and conclusion.
9. Check the audience challenge and solution release schedule.
10. Approve the final episode manuscript.

The episode should read as one coherent narrative rather than ten unrelated stories.

## 11.3 Production derivatives

The approved episode can become the source for additional creative outputs.

```text
APPROVED EPISODE
│
├── COVER GRAPHIC
├── TITLE GRAPHIC
├── COMIC
├── VIDEO
├── TRAILER
├── AUDIO
├── SOCIAL CONTENT
├── SERVICE CARDS
└── ARCHIVE PACKAGE
```

Not every output must be produced for every episode.

The production manifest should record which deliverables are required, optional, deferred or cancelled.

## 11.4 Production dependency rule

Where one output depends on another, the dependency must be respected.

For example:

```text
APPROVED CHRONICLE
       │
       ▼
APPROVED EPISODE
       │
       ├──► COMIC SCRIPT
       │
       ├──► VIDEO SCRIPT
       │
       ├──► TRAILER
       │
       ├──► AUDIO SCRIPT
       │
       └──► SOCIAL CONTENT
```

A derivative must not introduce unapproved narrative developments.

Any material creative deviation must be returned for editorial review.

---

# 12. AUDIENCE PARTICIPATION

## 12.1 Audience challenge

Each daily chronicle should conclude with an audience challenge.

The challenge should be recorded in the continuity and production records.

The solution must remain restricted until its scheduled release.

## 12.2 Challenge lifecycle

```text
CHALLENGE CREATED
       │
       ▼
EDITORIAL REVIEW
       │
       ▼
PUBLISHED
       │
       ▼
AUDIENCE PARTICIPATION
       │
       ▼
SOLUTION RELEASE
       │
       ▼
ARCHIVE
```

The system should distinguish between public challenge material and restricted answer material.

## 12.3 Participation and canon

Audience participation can influence approved creative opportunities, but it does not independently establish canon.

Subscription, course participation, crowdfunding or other forms of participation must not grant automatic authority to override editorial standards or established canon.

---

# 13. REPOSITORY AND FILE ARCHITECTURE

## 13.1 Repository principle

The existing Classies Chronicles repository should remain the primary project repository unless a formal decision establishes otherwise.

The architecture should integrate with the existing repository rather than create a competing archive.

## 13.2 Proposed document location

The following is a proposed logical arrangement, not an instruction to reorganise the existing repository immediately.

```text
Classies_Chronicles/
│
├── GOVERNANCE/
│   ├── CC-MPADIS/
│   ├── DOCUMENT_REGISTER/
│   ├── DECISION_REGISTER/
│   └── CHANGE_CONTROL/
│
├── FRAMEWORKS/
│   ├── MASTER_EPISODE_FRAMEWORK/
│   └── DAILY_CHRONICLE_GENERATION_PROTOCOL/
│
├── PRODUCTION_RECORDS/
│   ├── EPISODE_CONTINUITY/
│   ├── PRODUCTION_MANIFESTS/
│   └── HANDOVER_RECORDS/
│
├── CC_AI_DESK/
│   ├── CANON/
│   ├── AI_DESK_ARCHITECTURE/
│   └── PRODUCTION_RESOURCES/
│
├── STUDIO_REFERENCES/
│   └── STUDIO_STRUCTURE_BLUEPRINT/
│
├── RESEARCH/
│   ├── NEWS/
│   ├── HISTORY/
│   ├── SCIENCE/
│   ├── BIBLE_STUDY/
│   └── HEALTH_SAFETY/
│
├── PRODUCTION_ASSETS/
│   ├── GRAPHICS/
│   ├── COMICS/
│   ├── VIDEO/
│   └── AUDIO/
│
├── SEASONS/
│   └── SEASON_AND_EPISODE_ARCHIVES/
│
└── RELEASES/
    ├── APPROVED_EPISODES/
    └── PUBLICATION_PACKAGES/
```

## 13.3 Existing structure preservation

Before creating directories or moving files:

1. Review the existing repository.
2. Identify current folders and naming conventions.
3. Identify existing files with overlapping responsibilities.
4. Map existing files to the proposed architecture.
5. Identify duplicate or obsolete material.
6. Prepare a migration proposal.
7. Obtain approval before making structural changes.

No file should be deleted merely because a proposed architecture does not include its current location.

## 13.4 File naming

A consistent naming convention should be adopted after the existing repository conventions have been reviewed.

Proposed document naming:

`Classies_Chronicles_[Document_Name]_v[Major.Minor.Patch].md`

Proposed episode identifiers should retain the established season, chapter/phase and episode references where applicable.

Daily records should use stable identifiers independent of their display titles.

Example:

`CC-S00-TP01-E01-D01`

This example illustrates an identifier format only. It does not establish a new official identifier scheme.

---

# 14. IDENTIFIERS AND DATA MODEL

## 14.1 Purpose

The production architecture should use stable identifiers to connect documents and production records.

This makes future automation possible without requiring a database immediately.

## 14.2 Proposed record identifiers

| Record type      | Proposed identifier |
| ---------------- | ------------------- |
| Season           | CC-Sxx              |
| Chapter/term     | CC-Sxx-TPxx         |
| Episode          | CC-Sxx-TPxx-Exx     |
| Daily chronicle  | CC-Sxx-TPxx-Exx-Dxx |
| Character        | CC-CHAR-xxxxx       |
| Location         | CC-LOC-xxxxx        |
| Mission          | CC-MSN-xxxxx        |
| Storyline        | CC-STY-xxxxx        |
| Research source  | CC-SRC-xxxxx        |
| Production asset | CC-AST-xxxxx        |
| Decision         | CC-DEC-xxxxx        |
| Change request   | CC-CR-xxxxx         |

These identifiers are proposed conventions.

They should be confirmed against existing identifiers before adoption.

## 14.3 Minimum common metadata

Every major production record should contain, where applicable:

```text
Record ID:
Record Type:
Title:
Status:
Version:
Created:
Last Updated:
Owner:
Related Episode:
Related Chronicle:
Source References:
Approval Status:
Notes:
```

## 14.4 Relationship model

A daily chronicle may reference multiple characters, locations, research sources and missions.

A production asset may reference one or more approved chronicles.

An episode may contain multiple daily chronicles.

A decision may affect several documents.

These relationships should be recorded using stable identifiers rather than relying exclusively on file names.

---

# 15. VERSION CONTROL AND CHANGE MANAGEMENT

## 15.1 Version control

The project should use a consistent versioning approach.

A proposed semantic version pattern is:

`MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`

* MAJOR: Significant architectural or structural change.
* MINOR: New functionality or approved requirements.
* PATCH: Corrections and clarifications that do not change intended behaviour.

The final versioning convention should be reconciled with existing repository practices.

## 15.2 Change requests

A change request should contain:

* Change ID.
* Requesting party.
* Affected document or resource.
* Existing condition.
* Proposed change.
* Reason.
* Impact assessment.
* Related dependencies.
* Approval decision.
* Implementation status.
* Version references.

## 15.3 Change impact assessment

Before approving a change, assess its effect on:

* Master framework.
* Daily generation protocol.
* Continuity records.
* Character canon.
* Studio responsibilities.
* Production templates.
* Existing episodes.
* Archives.
* Publication outputs.
* Future automation.

## 15.4 Historical preservation

Superseded documents should remain available for historical reference.

A revision must not erase the record of why an earlier decision was made.

---

# 16. AI DESK AND MULTI-PLATFORM INTEGRATION

## 16.1 AI-independent architecture

The production architecture should not depend on one AI provider.

Compatible AI systems may contribute to:

* Research.
* Chronicle generation.
* Continuity analysis.
* Script development.
* Graphic and comic planning.
* Editorial review.
* Production documentation.

## 16.2 Shared operating context

Each AI contribution should receive the relevant current resources:

1. Applicable Master Episode Framework.
2. Daily Generation Protocol.
3. Previous chronicle.
4. Current episode continuity ledger.
5. Relevant canon references.
6. Relevant research sources.
7. Current production manifest, where production work is requested.
8. Any applicable human editorial instructions.

The AI must identify missing or inaccessible resources.

## 16.3 AI handover

The handover summary should contain:

* Current episode and day.
* Current storyline.
* Important recent developments.
* Character status.
* Mission status.
* Established facts.
* Proposed developments.
* Unresolved issues.
* Restricted information.
* Next production requirement.

This is the minimum information needed to reduce continuity loss when moving between AI platforms.

## 16.4 AI authority limitations

AI systems may recommend changes.

They may not independently approve:

* New canon.
* Major character outcomes.
* Organisational restructuring.
* Changes to the production framework.
* Changes to external systems.

Human approval remains the controlling authority.

---

# 17. QUALITY ASSURANCE AND RELEASE CONTROL

## 17.1 Quality gates

The proposed production process contains six principal gates.

| Gate                     | Purpose                             | Required result              |
| ------------------------ | ----------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| G1 — Source validation   | Confirm applicable source material  | Sources identified           |
| G2 — Chronicle review    | Check narrative and factual quality | Chronicle ready for approval |
| G3 — Continuity review   | Check consistency                   | Continuity updated           |
| G4 — Episode compilation | Assemble ten chronicles             | Complete episode draft       |
| G5 — Production review   | Validate derivatives                | Deliverables approved        |
| G6 — Release review      | Verify final package                | Publication authorised       |

## 17.2 Editorial checks

The reviewer should check:

* Story coherence.
* Character consistency.
* Temporal consistency.
* Location accuracy.
* Research accuracy.
* Educational accuracy.
* Scripture references.
* Fictional disclaimers where necessary.
* Narrative pacing.
* Repetition.
* Audience challenge integrity.
* Production completeness.

## 17.3 Release control

Only authorised personnel should mark an episode or derivative as approved for publication.

A draft, generated asset or internally reviewed file is not automatically a published release.

The release record should identify:

* Approved item.
* Version.
* Approval date.
* Approver.
* Publication destination.
* Publication date.
* Archive location.

---

# 18. ARCHIVING AND LONG-TERM KNOWLEDGE

## 18.1 Archive principle

Completed production material must remain accessible for future continuity and research.

Archives are not merely storage folders.

They are part of the project's long-term knowledge system.

## 18.2 Episode archive package

A completed episode package should include, as applicable:

```text
EPISODE_ARCHIVE/
│
├── FINAL_EPISODE/
│
├── DAILY_CHRONICLES/
│
├── CONTINUITY_LEDGER/
│
├── PRODUCTION_MANIFEST/
│
├── HANDOVER_SUMMARY/
│
├── COMICS/
│
├── GRAPHICS/
│
├── VIDEO/
│
├── AUDIO/
│
├── RESEARCH_REFERENCES/
│
├── APPROVAL_RECORDS/
│
└── RELEASE_INFORMATION/
```

## 18.3 Archive integrity

Where appropriate, the archive should preserve:

* Original generated material.
* Human-edited material.
* Approved final versions.
* Relevant source references.
* Version history.
* Approval records.
* Production assets.

The archive should make it possible to identify which version was actually published.

---

# 19. FUTURE AUTOMATION AND TECHNICAL INTEGRATION

## 19.1 Design principle

The initial implementation should use the existing repository and lightweight document records.

A database or complex production platform should not be introduced until actual production demands justify it.

## 19.2 Staged technical development

### Stage 1 — Controlled document operations

Use:

* Markdown and text documents.
* Git version control.
* Stable record identifiers.
* Episode ledgers.
* Production manifests.
* Manual approval.

This stage establishes the operating model.

### Stage 2 — Repository validation

Introduce optional automation to check:

* Required files.
* Missing metadata.
* Duplicate identifiers.
* Broken internal references.
* Missing source links.
* Inconsistent episode naming.
* Incomplete production manifests.

Node.js or PowerShell would be suitable implementation options.

### Stage 3 — Production assistance

Potential tools may assist with:

* Episode package creation.
* Daily handover preparation.
* Production status reports.
* Asset inventory.
* Archive verification.
* Source register maintenance.

These should initially remain human-supervised.

### Stage 4 — Structured data integration

If the volume of records grows sufficiently, consider a structured data store.

Potential records include:

* Episodes.
* Chronicles.
* Characters.
* Locations.
* Missions.
* Research sources.
* Production assets.
* Decisions.
* Approvals.

The database should support the existing document architecture rather than replace the human-readable records without justification.

### Stage 5 — Integrated production dashboard

A future dashboard could display:

* Current production position.
* Active episode.
* Daily chronicle status.
* Continuity alerts.
* Outstanding editorial decisions.
* Production deliverables.
* Publication schedule.
* Archive status.

This is a future option, not an immediate implementation requirement.

## 19.3 Automation safeguards

Automation must not:

* Create canon without approval.
* Publish unapproved material.
* Change the Studio Blueprint.
* Modify external systems without authorisation.
* Invent missing temporal information.
* Replace an existing approved file without preserving its history.

---

# 20. IMPLEMENTATION ROADMAP

## Phase 1 — Document reconciliation

**Objective:** Establish the authoritative document set.

Tasks:

1. Confirm the current Master Episode Framework version and approval status.
2. Confirm the Daily Chronicle Generation Protocol version and status.
3. Confirm the CC-ECLPM version and status.
4. Obtain the Studio Structure Skeletal Blueprint files.
5. Review the AI Desk architecture.
6. Review the current repository structure.
7. Create a document register.
8. Record unresolved conflicts.

**Exit condition:** Document relationships and outstanding decisions are documented.

## Phase 2 — Architecture approval

**Objective:** Approve the production architecture.

Tasks:

1. Review CC-MPADIS.
2. Resolve the annual hierarchy conflict.
3. Confirm document responsibilities.
4. Confirm continuity and manifest separation.
5. Confirm the studio integration approach.
6. Confirm naming and identifiers.
7. Record approval decisions.

**Exit condition:** The architecture has formal approval.

## Phase 3 — Controlled operational test

**Objective:** Test the document architecture without introducing unnecessary software.

Tasks:

1. Select one controlled episode.
2. Prepare its continuity ledger.
3. Prepare its production manifest.
4. Generate daily chronicles using CC-DCGP.
5. Test an AI handover.
6. Compile the episode.
7. Track derivative production.
8. Review the archive package.
9. Record problems and improvements.

**Exit condition:** The complete document workflow has been tested.

## Phase 4 — Standardisation

**Objective:** Incorporate approved improvements.

Tasks:

1. Update affected documents.
2. Resolve naming inconsistencies.
3. Publish approved templates.
4. Establish the document register.
5. Establish the decision and change registers.
6. Update the production instructions.
7. Record the approved operating procedure.

**Exit condition:** The production system has a consistent operational baseline.

## Phase 5 — Selective automation

**Objective:** Reduce repetitive administrative work.

Tasks:

1. Identify the most time-consuming manual tasks.
2. Select low-risk automation opportunities.
3. Build repository validation tools.
4. Test package generation.
5. Test continuity and metadata checks.
6. Document limitations.
7. Obtain approval before deployment.

**Exit condition:** Automation demonstrably improves production without weakening editorial control.

# 24. ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA

CC-MPADIS may be considered ready for approval when:

1. Its relationship with the Master Episode Framework is clear.
2. Its relationship with CC-DCGP is clear.
3. Its relationship with CC-ECLPM is clear.
4. The Studio Blueprint has been reconciled.
5. The annual production hierarchy has been confirmed.
6. The repository structure has been reviewed.
7. Canon authority is clearly established.
8. Production and continuity records remain separate.
9. Human approval requirements are explicit.
10. The implementation roadmap is practical.
11. No proposed requirement is misrepresented as an existing approved decision.
12. The architecture can be tested without requiring premature software development.

---

# 25. FINAL ARCHITECTURAL POSITION

The proposed Classies Chronicles production system should be built around five essential capabilities:

**1. Govern**

Maintain a controlled set of authoritative documents, decisions and approval records.

**2. Generate**

Use a reusable, AI-independent protocol to produce one daily chronicle at a time.

**3. Remember**

Maintain a continuity ledger that preserves the evolving universe across days, episodes and seasons.

**4. Produce**

Use a separate production manifest to coordinate editing, comics, graphics, video, audio and publication.

**5. Preserve**

Maintain reliable archives and source references so the project can continue across different AI systems, contributors and production periods.

The architecture must remain modular.

The Master Episode Framework should not become a continuity database. The Daily Chronicle Generation Protocol should not become a canon repository. The Studio Blueprint should not be replaced by an independently invented organisational structure. The Episode Continuity Ledger should not become a production checklist.

Each resource should perform its intended function and communicate with the others through clearly defined references and records.

**The intended result is a continuous, repository-driven, AI-independent creative production ecosystem, governed by human approval and supported by traceable documents, consistent continuity and controlled production workflows.**

---

## Architectural observations and next steps

There are three important matters to resolve before implementation:

1. **Annual structure conflict:** The original proposal describes one season per year with five chapters/terms. The repository's v0.01.09 framework describes five seasons per year. This is a material scheduling difference, not just a naming issue. It affects episode numbering, archive structure and the Millennium Probe integration.

2. **Studio Blueprint reconciliation:** The compiled proposals themselves identify the need to reconcile the Studio Blueprint, and the existing repository's source-and-decision log references Blueprint v23.0 and AI Desk Architecture v22.0. Those exact files need to be examined before we can confirm studio roles and responsibilities.

3. **Document authority:** The repository's Master Episode Framework v0.01.09 is marked *Refactored Functional Candidate — Pending Human Approval*. It should not be treated as formally approved merely because it is present in the repository. ([GitHub][1])

**My recommendation:** Keep CC-MPADIS at Proposed v0.01.00 for now. The next useful deliverable is a document reconciliation matrix that compares the actual CC-MEGF, CC-DCGP, CC-ECLPM, Studio Blueprint and AI Desk files—identifying overlaps, contradictions, missing responsibilities and the exact changes required before approval.

[1]: https://github.com/cupidblack/Classies_Chronicles/blob/main/Classies_Chronicles_Master_Episode_Framework_v0.01.09.txt "Classies_Chronicles/Classies_Chronicles_Master_Episode_Framework_v0.01.09.txt at main · cupidblack/Classies_Chronicles · GitHub"

Prepare the document reconciliation matrix that compares the actual CC-MEGF, CC-DCGP, CC-ECLPM, Studio Blueprint and AI Desk files—identifying overlaps, contradictions, missing responsibilities and the exact changes required before approval.  

The reconciliation has surfaced several material issues that should be resolved before approving CC-MPADIS. The most significant are:

* **Calendar architecture:** the original proposal and compiled framework use one season with five chapters per year; the repository's current CC-MEGF v0.01.09 uses five seasons per year.
* **Document authority:** the current CC-MEGF is a functional candidate pending human approval, not an approved standard.
* **Archive architecture:** CC-MEGF's proposed folder structure does not fully align with the more detailed Studio Blueprint v22.2 and AI Desk Architecture v22.0.
* **Continuity ownership:** the AI Desk, canon repository and episode archive need clearer boundaries.
* **Operational documents:** CC-DCGP and CC-ECLPM have been drafted in our conversation, but I could not verify them as committed repository files. They must be treated as draft documents until formally versioned and placed in the repository.

I was able to examine the original episode proposal, compiled AI contributions, CC-MEGF v0.01.09, Studio Blueprint v22.2, AI Desk Architecture v22.0 and the existing Cupid Black character canon. The Blueprint directory's later versions, including the previously referenced v23.0, were not retrievable, so I have not assumed that v22.2 is the latest version.

Here is the controlled reconciliation document.

## 1.1 Overall assessment

The documents are substantially compatible in their architectural intentions.

They share several important principles:

1. A continuous storytelling universe.
2. A ten-working-day episode production model.
3. One daily chronicle at a time.
4. Human editorial authority.
5. Persistent narrative continuity.
6. AI-provider independence.
7. One persistent AI Desk.
8. Episode-owned production records.
9. Controlled research and source attribution.
10. Separation of production assets from reusable intelligence infrastructure.

The main problem is not a fundamental disagreement about the purpose of Classies Chronicles.

The problem is that these principles have been expressed in different documents at different levels of detail, with some requirements repeated, omitted or contradicted.

The documents should not duplicate each other's detailed instructions.

**CC-DCGP:** Applies those requirements to one daily chronicle.

**CC-ECLPM:** Maintains the episode's continuity and production records.

**Studio Blueprint:** Defines the studio's organisational and technical production environment.

**AI Desk Architecture:** Defines the persistent AI intelligence, retrieval and operating environment.

CC-MPADIS coordinates these relationships.

# 3. DETAILED RECONCILIATION MATRIX

## 3.1 Calendar and production hierarchy

**Finding ID:** R-001
**Severity:** CRITICAL

### Source comparison

Original proposal v0.01.05:

* One season per year.
* Five chapters/terms per year.
* Eight weeks per chapter.
* Two weeks per episode.
* One day per daily part.

Compiled framework:

* One season per year.
* Five chapters per year.
* Four two-week episodes per chapter.
* Ten working days per episode.

Repository CC-MEGF v0.01.09:

* Five seasons per year.
* Approximately two months per season.
* Four broad movements within each season.
* Approximately two-week phases.
* Ten working days per episode.

### Conflict

The annual hierarchy is materially different.

The difference affects:

* Season numbering.
* Chapter/term numbering.
* Episode numbering.
* Episode archive names.
* Production calendar calculations.
* Millennium Probe integration.
* Annual story planning.

### Required correction

**CR-001 — Annual hierarchy reconciliation**

The project owner must confirm one authoritative annual hierarchy.

The decision must explicitly define:

1. Number of seasons per year.
2. Number of chapters or terms per season.
3. Duration of each chapter or term.
4. Number of episodes per chapter or term.
5. Whether episodes must begin on Mondays.
6. Whether every episode must contain exactly ten working days.
7. How incomplete phase boundaries are handled.
8. Whether the calendar is a production schedule, narrative calendar or both.

No automated episode calculation should be implemented until this decision is recorded.

### Recommendation

Retain the original one-season/five-chapter proposal as a candidate because it is explicitly connected to the supplied Millennium Probe phase-boundary concept.

However, this is a recommendation for review, not an approved decision.

---

## 3.2 Episode numbering and identity

**Finding ID:** R-002
**Severity:** CRITICAL

### Source comparison

CC-MEGF v0.01.09 identifies an existing archive file:

`Classies Chronicles, Episode 0.00.10-The Signal Before Sunrise`

It also discusses a proposed logical identifier:

`S00E01`

The AI Desk Architecture v22.0 uses logical episode IDs such as:

`S00E01`

The original proposal uses a hierarchy resembling:

`S00_TP01_E01`

The previous CC-ECLPM draft proposed a more elaborate identifier:

`CC-S00-TP01-E01-D01`

### Conflict

The same production item may be represented by several different identifiers.

This creates a risk of:

* Duplicate records.
* Incorrect episode mapping.
* Broken references.
* Conflicting archive paths.
* AI retrieval errors.

### Required correction

**CR-002 — Canonical identifier specification**

Create one approved identifier standard.

It should define:

* Season ID.
* Chapter/term ID.
* Episode ID.
* Daily chronicle ID.
* Scene ID.
* Asset ID.
* Mission ID.
* Character ID.
* Research source ID.

Existing published filenames must not be renamed until their canonical mapping has been established.

### Recommendation

Use logical identifiers independently of folder names and display titles.

For example:

`S00E01-D01`

The final scheme must preserve compatibility with the existing AI Desk's logical episode reference model.

---

## 3.3 Daily chronicle length and episode duration

**Finding ID:** R-010
**Severity:** MEDIUM

### Source comparison

Original proposal:

* Daily chronicle target: approximately 160 seconds.
* Episode target: approximately 20 minutes.
* Episode range: 15–30 minutes.

Compiled contributions:

* Daily chronicle target: approximately 3–5 minutes.

CC-MEGF v0.01.09:

* Daily chronicle target: approximately 3–4 minutes.

Previous conversational generation instructions:

* Approximately 3–4 minutes.

### Conflict

The original duration target does not align cleanly with the later daily reading target.

Ten daily chronicles of 3–4 minutes would represent approximately 30–40 minutes of reading before editorial compression.

The compiled episode's 20-minute target is therefore not automatically compatible with the daily reading target.

### Required correction

**CR-003 — Editorial duration standard**

Confirm whether the 20-minute episode target refers to:

* Spoken production.
* Video runtime.
* Audio runtime.
* Edited episode narrative.
* A target independent of the daily reading duration.

The daily chronicle length and compiled episode duration must be specified separately.

### Recommendation

Maintain 3–4 minutes as the provisional daily reading target.

Do not impose a compulsory 20-minute final episode runtime until the intended publication format is confirmed.

---

## 3.4 Daily generation protocol

**Finding ID:** R-005
**Severity:** HIGH

### Source comparison

CC-MEGF defines:

* Six-stage Eternal Dreamfall workflow.
* Research methodology.
* Daily output format.
* Source validation.
* Narrative requirements.
* Quality gates.

CC-DCGP provides operational instructions for applying the framework to one daily chronicle.

### Overlap

Both documents contain:

* Resource retrieval.
* Date validation.
* Millennium Timezone checks.
* Bible study.
* YouVersion verse retrieval.
* Health & Safety research.
* News research.
* Continuity checks.
* Chronicle generation.
* Editorial review.

### Risk

If the same procedural instructions are maintained independently in both documents, future revisions may create contradictory requirements.

### Required correction

**CR-004 — Generation protocol boundary**

CC-MEGF must define the mandatory generation principles.

CC-DCGP must define the execution procedure.

The protocol should reference the framework rather than reproduce it.

### Specific changes to CC-DCGP

1. Add document ID and version.
2. Add source-framework version.
3. Add required resource register.
4. Add source-access reporting.
5. Add Millennium Probe validation.
6. Add date and episode-position calculation.
7. Add daily chronicle output schema.
8. Add continuity-update schema.
9. Add audience-challenge requirement, subject to confirmation.
10. Add human approval boundary.
11. Add AI handover requirements.
12. Add failure handling for unavailable resources.

The protocol must not introduce new story requirements that are absent from the approved framework.

## 3.5 Continuity ledger and production manifest

**Finding ID:** R-008
**Severity:** MEDIUM

### Source comparison

CC-MEGF defines continuity principles and minimum persistent records.

AI Desk Architecture v22.0 defines episode-owned information and an episode context package.

Studio Blueprint v22.2 defines the episode archive.

CC-ECLPM defines the episode continuity ledger and production manifest.

### Overlap

All four resources address some combination of:

* Episode metadata.
* Storyline notes.
* Character status.
* Mission records.
* Research.
* Production requirements.
* Assets.
* Publication status.
* Post-episode analysis.

### Risk

Creating independent records without a clear authoritative location may result in duplicated or contradictory information.

### Required correction

**CR-005 — Continuity and manifest separation**

Maintain two distinct operational responsibilities:

**Episode Continuity Ledger**

Records what happened in the story.

**Episode Production Manifest**

Records what was produced and its operational status.

The two records should be linked through stable episode and chronicle identifiers.

### Proposed integration

The ECL should align with:

`01_EPISODE_CONTEXT/04_CONTINUITY_REFERENCE.md`

The EPM should align with:

`01_EPISODE_CONTEXT/11_PRODUCTION_REQUIREMENTS.md`

Additional detailed records may be stored in:

`10_ARCHIVE/`

The precise file allocation must be checked against the full AI Desk schema before implementation.

### Required ECL fields

* Episode ID.
* Daily chronicle ID.
* Story date.
* Character developments.
* Location developments.
* Mission status.
* Temporal coordinates.
* Equipment status.
* Events and discoveries.
* Decisions.
* Canon proposals.
* Unresolved storylines.
* Audience challenge reference.
* Source references.
* Next-day handover.

### Required EPM fields

* Asset ID.
* Episode ID.
* Deliverable type.
* Source chronicle.
* Production owner.
* Production status.
* Revision.
* Approval status.
* Consent status, where applicable.
* Licensing status, where applicable.
* Publication status.
* Archive reference.

---

## 3.6 Studio Blueprint and archive architecture

**Finding ID:** R-003
**Severity:** HIGH

### Source comparison

Studio Blueprint v22.2 establishes:

* One persistent AI Desk.
* Episode-owned data.
* Separate production and communications functions.
* A standard episode archive.
* Human editorial authority.
* Controlled consent and licensing.
* Provider-neutral architecture.

Its episode archive contains:

```text
EPISODE/
│
├── 01_EPISODE_CONTEXT/
├── 02_RAW_VIDEO/
├── 03_RAW_AUDIO/
├── 04_PROJECT_FILES/
├── 05_STORY_AND_SCRIPT/
├── 06_GRAPHICS/
├── 07_GENERATED_MEDIA/
├── 08_EDITED_MEDIA/
├── 09_PUBLISHING/
└── 10_ARCHIVE/
```

CC-MEGF proposes a different logical structure for episode archives.

CC-ECLPM also proposes its own archive structure.

### Conflict

There are three proposed archive arrangements.

The Studio Blueprint and AI Desk Architecture provide the most detailed existing structural contract.

Creating another competing archive layout would introduce unnecessary complexity.

### Required correction

**CR-006 — Archive structure authority**

The approved Studio Blueprint and AI Desk Architecture should govern the physical and logical episode archive structure.

CC-MEGF, CC-DCGP and CC-ECLPM should use that structure rather than invent independent folder hierarchies.

### Specific changes

1. Replace the independent CC-MEGF archive proposal with a reference to the approved archive contract.
2. Remove duplicate archive trees from CC-ECLPM.
3. Place continuity and production records within the existing episode context/archive contract.
4. Preserve the distinction between story/script files and production records.
5. Retain the episode archive as the authoritative source of episode-specific information.

### Outstanding issue

The Studio Blueprint directory contains a reference to later Blueprint work.

Version v22.2 has been reviewed, but later-version precedence remains unverified.

No final structural changes should be made until that review is completed.

---

## 3.7 AI Desk ownership and episode data

**Finding ID:** R-004
**Severity:** HIGH

### Source comparison

AI Desk Architecture v22.0 establishes:

* One permanent CC_AI_DESK.
* Episode-specific information belongs to episode archives.
* Retrieval is scope-controlled.
* Indexes are rebuildable.
* Persona evolution requires approval.
* Consent and licensing control access.
* AI Desk and episode archives remain separate.

Studio Blueprint v22.2 repeats these principles.

CC-MEGF v0.01.09 proposes a reusable AI Desk and episode archive structure.

### Overlap

The documents agree on the fundamental architecture.

### Ambiguity

The existing Classies Chronicles repository contains:

`CC_AI_DESK/04_CANON/CHARACTERS/`

The Cupid Black Character Canon identifies itself as a reusable character reference for every episode.

This raises an ownership question.

Is approved franchise canon:

1. Part of the persistent AI Desk?
2. A separate franchise-level canon repository referenced by the AI Desk?
3. A shared source library managed outside both the AI Desk and individual episode archives?

The AI Desk Architecture does not fully define this distinction.

### Required correction

**CR-007 — Canon ownership and retrieval**

Define three separate categories:

**A. Franchise-level reusable knowledge**

Examples:

* Approved character canon.
* Established locations.
* Technology rules.
* Organisational relationships.
* Global timeline rules.

**B. AI Desk intelligence infrastructure**

Examples:

* Persona.
* Prompts.
* Runtime configuration.
* Retrieval configuration.
* Source catalog.
* Search indexes.
* Operational logs.

**C. Episode-specific information**

Examples:

* Episode research.
* Daily chronicles.
* Mission developments.
* Temporary character states.
* Episode-specific assets.
* Episode decisions.

### Recommendation

Do not automatically relocate existing character canon.

Instead, formally define the franchise canon repository as an authoritative source that the AI Desk indexes and retrieves under controlled permissions.

This preserves the existing character-canon location while avoiding the AI Desk becoming a permanent duplicate episode archive.

---

## 3.8 AI Desk folder structure

**Finding ID:** R-003A
**Severity:** HIGH

### Source comparison

CC-MEGF proposes:

```text
CC_AI_DESK/
├── 00_MASTER_CONTEXT/
├── 01_CANON_AND_CONTINUITY/
├── 02_CHARACTER_DATABASE/
├── 03_ORGANISATIONAL_STRUCTURE/
├── 04_TIMELINE_AND_TIMEZONE/
...
```

AI Desk Architecture v22.0 proposes:

```text
CC_AI_DESK/
├── 00_SYSTEM/
├── 01_PERSONA/
├── 02_PROMPTS/
├── 03_SCHEMAS/
├── 04_CATALOG/
├── 05_INDICES/
├── 06_CONFIG/
├── 07_SCRIPTS/
├── 08_UI/
├── 09_CONNECTORS/
├── 10_LOGS/
└── 11_BACKUPS/
```

### Conflict

These structures assign different meanings to the same numbered directories.

### Required correction

**CR-008 — AI Desk filesystem authority**

The detailed AI Desk filesystem structure must be governed by AI Desk Architecture v22.0 or its approved successor.

CC-MEGF must not redefine the AI Desk directory structure.

Franchise canon and reusable knowledge should be linked to the AI Desk through the approved catalog and retrieval interface.

### Required changes

1. Remove competing AI Desk directory definitions from CC-MEGF.
2. Update CC-MPADIS to reference the AI Desk Architecture.
3. Define the canon repository's logical source identity.
4. Define the AI Desk's retrieval reference to that source.
5. Preserve existing files until a migration plan is approved.

---

## 3.9 Character canon and continuity

**Finding ID:** R-004A
**Severity:** HIGH

### Source comparison

The Cupid Black Character Canon v0.10.0 establishes:

* Character identity and aliases.
* Personality.
* Dual-life identity rules.
* Ability progression.
* Foundation Phase restrictions.
* Ethical character principles.

CC-MEGF establishes the need to preserve character consistency.

CC-ECLPM tracks daily character developments.

### Overlap

The documents address character information at different levels.

### Required distinction

Character canon must contain established reusable character facts.

The continuity ledger must contain the character's changing state during an episode.

For example:

| Information                       | Correct record            |
| --------------------------------- | ------------------------- |
| Character identity                | Character canon           |
| Established alias                 | Character canon           |
| Established ability limitations   | Character canon           |
| Current emotional state           | Episode continuity ledger |
| Current location                  | Episode continuity ledger |
| Mission assignment                | Mission continuity record |
| New proposed ability              | Canon change proposal     |
| Approved permanent ability change | Character canon revision  |

### Required correction

**CR-009 — Character record boundary**

Add an explicit character-canon/episode-continuity distinction to CC-MEGF, CC-DCGP and CC-ECLPM.

No daily chronicle should silently rewrite a character's established profile.

---

## 3.10 Millennium Timezone and calendar interface

**Finding ID:** R-014
**Severity:** HIGH

### Source comparison

Original proposal v0.01.05 identifies:

`millennium-probe.php?probe=now`

It specifically references:

* `phase_start_iso`
* `phase_end_iso`

CC-MEGF v0.01.09 references two endpoints:

Historical:

`millennium-timezone.php?endpoint=historical`

Probe:

`millennium-timezone.php?endpoint=probe`

The AI Desk Architecture treats episode context and retrieval separately from the temporal endpoint implementation.

### Conflict

The documents describe different endpoint interfaces and different expected fields.

These may represent different versions or functions of the same wider temporal system.

They must not be assumed to be interchangeable.

### Required correction

**CR-010 — Temporal interface specification**

Create one authoritative temporal integration reference.

It must define:

* Endpoint URL.
* Endpoint purpose.
* HTTP method.
* Expected response format.
* Required fields.
* Optional fields.
* Retrieval timestamp.
* Timezone interpretation.
* Validation rules.
* Error handling.
* Historical and probe distinctions.
* Relationship to Sabbatical/Palmier data.

### Specific requirement

The Millennium Timezone must remain distinct from the Sabbatical/Palmier probe unless technical evidence establishes a relationship.

The previously discussed value:

`2167-04-30T01:13:07Z`

must not be substituted for a Millennium Timezone result without verification.

### Required changes

1. Update CC-MEGF with the approved endpoint contract.
2. Update CC-DCGP to use that contract.
3. Record the exact response used for each daily chronicle.
4. Add temporal source references to the continuity ledger.
5. Add endpoint failure handling.
6. Do not calculate episode position from guessed or substituted dates.

---

## 3.11 Bible study and Health & Safety integration

**Finding ID:** R-015
**Severity:** MEDIUM

### Source comparison

Original proposal establishes the sequence:

* Bible reading.
* YouVersion verse.
* Health & Safety lesson.

CC-MEGF expands this into a six-stage workflow.

AI Desk Architecture v22.0 provides separate episode context records for:

* Bible reference.
* Course lesson reference.

### Overlap

The documents agree that these materials should be integrated into the episode.

### Missing responsibility

There is insufficient distinction between:

* The original source material.
* The interpretation used in the narrative.
* The practical Health & Safety lesson.
* The actual Buzzjuice course/module reference.

### Required correction

**CR-011 — Educational source record**

Each episode should record:

1. Bible reading reference.
2. Reading-plan date.
3. YouVersion verse and translation.
4. Spiritual motif.
5. Health & Safety course reference.
6. Relevant lesson or module.
7. Practical application.
8. Source verification status.

The AI must not claim that a course lesson exists unless the course repository has been checked.

The spiritual reflection must not be presented as a substitute for professional safety guidance.

---

## 3.12 News and research management

**Finding ID:** R-016
**Severity:** MEDIUM

### Source comparison

CC-MEGF defines historical, current and fictional probe research streams.

AI Desk Architecture v22.0 places episode-specific headlines and research within the episode context.

CC-ECLPM provides a research register.

### Overlap

All three documents address research and source management.

### Required distinction

The AI Desk stores reusable research methodology and retrieval infrastructure.

The episode archive stores the research results actually used.

The continuity ledger records only research that affects the story.

### Required correction

**CR-012 — Research record boundary**

Maintain:

* Reusable research prompts in the AI Desk.
* Episode research results in the episode archive.
* Source provenance in the episode source register.
* Story consequences in the continuity ledger.

Historical, current and fictional probe material must remain separately labelled.

## 3.13 Service cards and audience challenges

**Finding ID:** R-017
**Severity:** HIGH

### Source comparison

Original proposal includes service-card use within the story.

CC-MEGF v0.01.09:

* Aims for four to five useful service cards per episode.
* Treats quality and relevance as more important than quota.
* Defines card metadata and publication states.
* Includes an audience interaction loop.

The compiled proposal recommends challenges or riddles in selected chronicles.

The previous conversational instructions establish an audience challenge at the end of each daily chronicle, with the answer revealed later.

### Conflict

The daily challenge requirement is stronger in the production instructions than in the published framework.

The published framework does not consistently define a challenge as mandatory for every daily chronicle.

### Required correction

**CR-013 — Audience participation rule**

Confirm the intended requirement.

If the daily challenge is mandatory, update CC-MEGF and CC-DCGP accordingly.

The challenge record must include:

* Challenge ID.
* Related chronicle.
* Challenge text.
* Challenge type.
* Publication status.
* Solution.
* Solution release date.
* Restricted/public status.

### Additional safeguard

The solution must remain restricted until its approved release.

Audience participation must not independently establish canon.

Service cards and audience challenges must remain separate deliverable types.

## 3.14 Multimedia production and publication

**Finding ID:** R-018
**Severity:** MEDIUM

### Source comparison

CC-MEGF v0.01.09 prioritises:

1. Daily text chronicle.
2. Cover/title image.
3. Comic.
4. Short video.
5. Interactive question/service card.
6. Podcast/audio later.
7. Full animation later.

Studio Blueprint v22.2 defines a wider media production system involving:

* OBS.
* REAPER.
* CAPCUT.
* Recording hardware.
* Communications.
* AI-generated media.
* Editing.
* Publishing.

CC-ECLPM tracks multiple production deliverables.

### Overlap

The documents all address production outputs but at different levels.

### Required correction

**CR-014 — Deliverable classification**

CC-MEGF should define creative priorities.

Studio Blueprint should define technical production methods.

CC-ECLPM should track individual deliverables.

CC-DCGP should not require the production of all possible derivatives.

### Required status model

Use a consistent set of production states, subject to confirmation:

* NOT_REQUIRED
* PLANNED
* DRAFT
* IN_PROGRESS
* REVIEW
* APPROVED
* PUBLISHED
* DEFERRED
* CANCELLED
* ARCHIVED

The status vocabulary should be standardised across production records.

## 3.15 Consent, licensing and privacy

**Finding ID:** R-019
**Severity:** HIGH

### Source comparison

Studio Blueprint v22.2 defines consent and licensing controls.

AI Desk Architecture v22.0 distinguishes retrieval permission from publication permission.

CC-MEGF includes privacy and confidentiality safeguards.

The previous CC-ECLPM draft tracks production assets and approval but does not fully adopt the AI Desk's permission model.

### Missing responsibility

A production asset can be technically complete but still not be eligible for retrieval or publication.

### Required correction

**CR-015 — Permission-state integration**

The EPM must distinguish:

* Asset production status.
* Retrieval eligibility.
* Consent status.
* Licensing status.
* Publication permission.
* Revocation status.

The AI Desk must not treat an indexed asset as automatically authorised for every use.

A withdrawn permission must override stale catalog or index data.

The Studio Blueprint's controlled Buzzjuice integration boundary must remain authoritative for participant information.
